  "content": "\"\"\"Build a test \"call\" from FLEURS with known speakers, to replay through the whole program.\n\n    python eval/make_test_call.py                  # needs eval/results/fleurs_speaker_pairs_full.csv\n    python -m livetr --file eval/data/test_call.wav --no-mic --no-color | tee eval/data/test_call_out.txt\n    python eval/score_call.py eval/data/test_call_out.txt\n\nFLEURS has no speaker ids. A reader is identified by a pair of recordings of the *same sentence* with ECAPA\nsimilarity >= 0.6: different readers never come close to that (max 0.30 for man/woman pairs, see\nfleurs_speakers.py), so such a pair is the same person recording twice. We take 2 women and 2 men, two turns\neach, in an order where every speaker returns after others have spoken. Two same-gender speakers per gender\nmake it a fair test (similar voices are the hard case).\n\nWrites eval/data/test_call.wav, test_call_opus16k.wav (through the 16 kbps Opus codec that calls use, needs\nffmpeg) and test_call.tsv (start, end, speaker, reference text).\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport csv\nimport io\nimport subprocess\nimport tarfile\nimport wave\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nfrom common import DATA, RESULTS\n\nGAP_S = 1.2\nORDER = [\"F1\", \"M1\", \"F2\", \"M2\", \"F1\", \"M2\", \"M1\", \"F2\"]   # every speaker comes back after others\n\n\ndef save(path: Path, audio: np.ndarray) -> None:\n    with wave.open(str(path), \"wb\") as w:\n        w.setnchannels(1)\n        w.setsampwidth(2)\n        w.setframerate(16000)\n        w.writeframes((np.clip(audio, -1, 1) * 32767).astype(np.int16).tobytes())\n\n\ndef main() -> None:\n    p = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n    p.add_argument(\"--min-sim\", type=float, default=0.6)\n    args = p.parse_args()\n\n    import soundfile as sf\n    from huggingface_hub import hf_hub_download\n\n    with open(RESULTS / \"fleurs_speaker_pairs_full.csv\") as f:\n        pairs = [r for r in csv.DictReader(f) if r[\"gender_a\"] == r[\"gender_b\"] and float(r[\"similarity\"]) >= args.min_sim]\n    pairs.sort(key=lambda r: -float(r[\"similarity\"]))\n    readers: dict[str, list[dict]] = {\"FEMALE\": [], \"MALE\": []}\n    used_sentences: set[str] = set()\n    for r in pairs:  # strongest pairs first, one reader per sentence, two per gender\n        g = r[\"gender_a\"]\n        if len(readers[g]) < 2 and r[\"sentence\"] not in used_sentences:\n            readers[g].append(r)\n            used_sentences.add(r[\"sentence\"])\n    if any(len(v) < 2 for v in readers.values()):\n        raise SystemExit(f\"not enough same-reader pairs >= {args.min_sim}: {[(k, len(v)) for k, v in readers.items()]}\")\n    speakers = {\"F1\": readers[\"FEMALE\"][0], \"F2\": readers[\"FEMALE\"][1], \"M1\": readers[\"MALE\"][0], \"M2\": readers[\"MALE\"][1]}\n\n    tsv = hf_hub_download(\"google/fleurs\", \"data/hy_am/test.tsv\", repo_type=\"dataset\")\n    with open(tsv, encoding=\"utf-8\") as f:\n        text = {r[1]: r[2] for r in csv.reader(f, delimiter=\"\\t\", quoting=csv.QUOTE_NONE)}\n    wanted = {s[k] for s in speakers.values() for k in (\"file_a\", \"file_b\")}\n    audio: dict[str, np.ndarray] = {}\n    archive = hf_hub_download(\"google/fleurs\", \"data/hy_am/audio/test.tar.gz\", repo_type=\"dataset\")\n    with tarfile.open(archive, \"r:gz\") as tar:\n        for m in tar:\n            name = Path(m.name).name\n            if m.isfile() and name in wanted:\n                a, sr = sf.read(io.BytesIO(tar.extractfile(m).read()), dtype=\"float32\")\n                if sr != 16000 or a.ndim != 1:\n                    raise ValueError(f\"{name}: expected 16 kHz mono\")\n                audio[name] = a\n\n    turn_of = {k: 0 for k in speakers}\n    parts, rows, t = [np.zeros(int(GAP_S * 16000), np.float32)], [], GAP_S\n    for spk in ORDER:\n        name = speakers[spk][\"file_a\" if turn_of[spk] == 0 else \"file_b\"]\n        turn_of[spk] += 1\n        a = audio[name]\n        rows.append((f\"{t:.2f}\", f\"{t + len(a) / 16000:.2f}\", spk, text[name]))\n        parts += [a, np.zeros(int(GAP_S * 16000), np.float32)]\n        t += len(a) / 16000 + GAP_S\n    call = np.concatenate(parts)\n    call *= 0.5 / max(np.abs(call).max(), 1e-6)  # same level for both versions\n\n    out = DATA / \"test_call.wav\"\n    save(out, call)\n    with open(DATA / \"test_call.tsv\", \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\", newline=\"\") as f:\n        w = csv.writer(f, delimiter=\"\\t\")\n        w.writerow([\"start\", \"end\", \"speaker\", \"reference\"])\n        w.writerows(rows)\n    opus = DATA / \"test_call_opus16k.wav\"\n    subprocess.run(f\"ffmpeg -y -loglevel error -i {out} -c:a libopus -b:a 16k -f ogg - | \"\n                   f\"ffmpeg -y -loglevel error -i - -ar 16000 -ac 1 -c:a pcm_s16le {opus}\", shell=True, check=True)\n    print(f\"{len(call) / 16000:.0f} s, {len(rows)} turns, speakers \"\n          + \", \".join(f\"{k}: sim {float(v['similarity']):.2f}\" for k, v in speakers.items()))\n    print(f\"wrote {out}, {opus}, {DATA / 'test_call.tsv'}\")\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n"
}

"""Build a test "call" from FLEURS with known speakers, to replay through the whole program.

    python eval/make_test_call.py                  # needs eval/results/fleurs_speaker_pairs_full.csv
    python -m livetr --file eval/data/test_call.wav --no-mic --no-color | tee eval/data/test_call_out.txt
    python eval/score_call.py eval/data/test_call_out.txt

FLEURS has no speaker ids. A reader is identified by two recordings with ECAPA similarity >= --min-sim (0.7):
different readers never come close to that (max 0.30 for man/woman pairs, see fleurs_speakers.py). We take 2 women and 2 men, two turns
... (see below).
FLEURS has few readers, so two such pairs can be the same person reading two sentences (a first version of this
test did exactly that: "two men" at similarity 0.74). A new reader is therefore only accepted if their voice is
below --max-cross (0.35, the level of man/woman pairs) against every reader already chosen. This makes the
ground truth reliable, but it also means the test does not contain two *very* similar voices.

Writes eval/data/test_call.wav, test_call_opus16k.wav (through the 16 kbps Opus codec that calls use, needs
ffmpeg) and test_call.tsv (start, end, speaker, reference text).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import csv
import io
import subprocess
import tarfile
import wave
```

The new model is now tested live, the results are written up in the README, and everything is pushed to GitHub (`2e5c63e`); local and GitHub match. Your mic also works now: −52 dB means it hears sound, where −90 dB was silence. Thanks for asking Sven about Claude vs Cowork; if his answer is in writing, keep that email.

## Results with the current model

| Brief asks | Result |
|---|---|
| Is the Armenian recognised? | ✅ 3.3 % character errors on single sentences, 5.8 % in a test call with long turns |
| Is the English understandable? | ✅ Mostly. Errors come from transcription mistakes, for example "civilization" heard as "politics". |
| Under 5 seconds? | ✅ **1.3 s typical, 1.6 s worst**, played live through BlackHole |
| Are speakers kept apart? | ✅ 3 of 3 people, 9 of 9 turns right, also through the Opus codec calls use |
| Local only? | ✅ Ran with all network access blocked |

Along the way I found that the free Armenian test data (FLEURS) has only **3 voices**: 1 man and 2 women. My first test call had used the same man twice by mistake. The program correctly treated him as one person.

## What I just did

- **Test call:** built from 3 known speakers and 9 turns, and run three ways: clean, through Opus, and **live through Multi-Output and BlackHole**.
- **Local only:** checked with the network blocked.
- **README, new section 6 "What works and what doesn't":** your talking points for the interview, with real examples of the weak spots. The 8 s cut split "քարանձավ" (cave) in two, so the cave disappeared from the English.
- **Other docs:** DECISIONS has all the numbers, and TESTS.md now ticks off everything that's verified.
- **`chats/`:** the live run is in `chats/program-output/`, and the chat exports are re-exported.

## Is everything good?

**For submitting:** the code, README and tests are ready. Three things are left, and only you can do them:
1. **Export the claude.ai sessions** (21 Oct plan and code, plus the project chat) into `chats/`.
2. **Give Sven access** to the private repo, or make it public.
3. **Reply to Sven's email** with the repo link, by **1 Nov, 23:59**.

**For the interview**, these aren't proven yet:

| Not proven | What to do | Time |
|---|---|---|
| **`Me` (your mic) in a real conversation** | Run `python -m livetr --mic "MacBook"` and speak Armenian for 1–2 minutes | 10 min |
| **Real people on a real call app** | One practice call: phone or friends on Meet/Zoom, with you speaking too | 30–60 min |
| **Fast, spontaneous speech** | The same practice call shows it. Only read speech has been tested with this model. | (same) |
| **Translations checked by a native speaker** | Read through the 24 sentences in `eval/sentences.tsv` and one output file | 30 min |

**On interview day:** plug in the charger, close Edge and other heavy apps, check that no other job is running, and select Multi-Output Device as the output, with headphones.

After the practice call, send me the output with `tee -i eval/data/practice_call.txt`. I'll check it and add it to `chats/program-output/` like the others.

### Assistant · 2026-10-23 22:56:07

There are three weak spots. Two can be fixed and measured with the tools we already have; the third needs people.

| Weak spot | Fix | How we'd measure it | Effort | Expected gain |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1. Long phrases cut every 8 s, splitting words** (`քարանձավ` → "…մի" + "Անցավ") | (a) Raise the limit from 8 s to about 15 s. ASR now takes only 0.2 s, so long phrases no longer threaten the 5 s budget. (b) Cut at the quietest moment near the limit instead of exactly at it. | Test call: CER today 5.7 %; whole sentences give 3.3 % | 1–2 h | High, and it's simple |
| **2. Colloquial translation** (`ինչ կա, չկա` → "there is no such thing") | Try the larger **NLLB 1.3B** model (same family, about 2× better known quality, about 0.8 GB more memory, about +0.5 s). Memory is now available, since the new ASR is small. | chrF against the official English FLEURS translations, plus a set of 20–30 colloquial sentences (you'd check those) | 2–3 h | Medium. Better overall, but no model handles Armenian slang well |
| **3. Two very similar voices** | No code fix is known to be needed yet. First it has to be tested: the FLEURS data has no such voices. | The practice call with 2 similar voices (e.g. two women) | your practice call | Shows whether it's a real problem |

I'd skip some other ideas:
- **Rewriting colloquial forms into literary Armenian** before translating (`էս` → `այս`, `ա` → `է`): it's fragile, because `ա` isn't always a verb.
- **A local LLM as the translator:** small models are weak in Armenian, and big ones don't fit in 8 GB next to everything else.

I'd do **1 first** (cheap, measurable, clear gain), then **2**. That costs about 3–5 hours of the roughly 11–15 left, which fits. The brief also says *"spend the time making the core more reliable rather than adding features"*, and these are exactly that.

```
"""Cut a continuous 16 kHz stream into utterances using a voice-activity probability per frame.

Pure logic: the VAD is passed in as a function, so this module is testable without any model.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

from collections import deque
from typing import Callable

import numpy as np

from livetr import SAMPLE_RATE
from livetr.types import Segment

VadFn = Callable[[np.ndarray], float]  # frame (float32, frame_size samples) -> speech probability 0..1


class Segmenter:
    def __init__(
        self,
        source: str,
        vad: VadFn,
        *,
        frame_size: int = 512,          # Silero VAD requires exactly 512 samples at 16 kHz (32 ms)
        threshold: float = 0.5,         # prob >= threshold starts speech
        neg_threshold: float | None = None,  # prob < neg_threshold counts as silence (hysteresis)
        min_silence_ms: int = 500,      # pause that ends an utterance
        speech_pad_ms: int = 160,       # audio kept before speech start and after speech end
        min_speech_ms: int = 400,       # shorter utterances are dropped (clicks, coughs)
        max_segment_s: float = 8.0,     # force a cut so ASR time stays bounded
    ):
        self.source = source
        self.vad = vad
        self.frame_size = frame_size
        self.frame_s = frame_size / SAMPLE_RATE
        self.threshold = threshold
        self.neg_threshold = threshold - 0.15 if neg_threshold is None else neg_threshold
        self.min_silence_frames = max(1, round(min_silence_ms / 1000 / self.frame_s))
        self.pad_frames = round(speech_pad_ms / 1000 / self.frame_s)
        self.min_speech_frames = round(min_speech_ms / 1000 / self.frame_s)
        self.max_frames = round(max_segment_s / self.frame_s)

        self.dropped_short = 0  # counter, reported in --stats
        self._buf = np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float32)
        self._pre: deque[np.ndarray] = deque(maxlen=max(self.pad_frames, 1))
        self._reset_segment()

    def _reset_segment(self) -> None:
        self._in_speech = False
        self._frames: list[np.ndarray] = []
        self._start_wall = 0.0
        self._speech_frames = 0
        self._silence_run = 0
        self._last_speech_idx = -1
        self._last_speech_end = 0.0

    def push(self, audio: np.ndarray, end_wall: float) -> list[Segment]:
        """Feed audio of any length. `end_wall` is the monotonic time of its last sample."""
        if audio.dtype != np.float32 or audio.ndim != 1:
            raise ValueError(f"expected 1-D float32 audio, got {audio.dtype} with shape {audio.shape}")
        self._buf = np.concatenate([self._buf, audio])
        n_frames = len(self._buf) // self.frame_size
        leftover = len(self._buf) - n_frames * self.frame_size
        out: list[Segment] = []
        for i in range(n_frames):
            frame = self._buf[i * self.frame_size:(i + 1) * self.frame_size]
            samples_after = (n_frames - i - 1) * self.frame_size + leftover
            frame_end = end_wall - samples_after / SAMPLE_RATE
            seg = self._step(frame, frame_end)
            if seg is not None:
                out.append(seg)
        self._buf = self._buf[n_frames * self.frame_size:].copy()
        return out

    def flush(self) -> list[Segment]:
        """End of stream: emit whatever speech is pending."""
        if not self._in_speech:
            return []
        seg = self._emit(forced=False)
        return [seg] if seg is not None else []

    def _step(self, frame: np.ndarray, frame_end: float) -> Segment | None:
        prob = self.vad(frame)
        if not self._in_speech:
            if prob >= self.threshold:
                self._in_speech = True
                self._frames = list(self._pre) + [frame]
                self._start_wall = frame_end - len(self._frames) * self.frame_s
                self._speech_frames = 1
                self._silence_run = 0
                self._last_speech_idx = len(self._frames) - 1
                self._last_speech_end = frame_end
                self._pre.clear()
            else:
                self._pre.append(frame)
            return None

        self._frames.append(frame)
        if prob >= self.neg_threshold:
            self._speech_frames += 1
            self._silence_run = 0
            self._last_speech_idx = len(self._frames) - 1
            self._last_speech_end = frame_end
        else:
            self._silence_run += 1

        if self._silence_run >= self.min_silence_frames:
            trailing = self._frames[self._last_speech_idx + 1:]
            seg = self._emit(forced=False)
            self._pre.extend(trailing)  # silence after the cut can pre-roll the next utterance
            return seg
        if len(self._frames) >= self.max_frames:
            # Cut at the current frame: the phrase is not over, but waiting longer breaks the latency budget.
            self._last_speech_idx = len(self._frames) - 1
            self._last_speech_end = frame_end
            seg = self._emit(forced=True)
            self._in_speech = True  # speech continues into the next segment
            self._start_wall = frame_end
            return seg
        return None

    def _emit(self, forced: bool) -> Segment | None:
        end_idx = self._last_speech_idx + 1 + (0 if forced else self.pad_frames)
        frames = self._frames[:end_idx]
        speech_frames = self._speech_frames
        start_wall, end_wall = self._start_wall, self._last_speech_end
        self._reset_segment()
        if speech_frames < self.min_speech_frames:
            self.dropped_short += 1
            return None
        return Segment(
            source=self.source,
            audio=np.concatenate(frames).astype(np.float32),
            start_wall=start_wall,
            end_wall=end_wall,
            forced_cut=forced,
        )


class SileroVAD:
    """Silero VAD wrapper. Stateful: use one instance per audio source."""

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        import torch
        from silero_vad import load_silero_vad

        self._torch = torch
        self._model = load_silero_vad()

    def __call__(self, frame: np.ndarray) -> float:
        with self._torch.no_grad():
            return float(self._model(self._torch.from_numpy(frame), SAMPLE_RATE).item())

```
Useful knobs: `--min-silence-ms` (pause that ends a phrase, default 500), `--max-segment-s` (forced cut,
default 8), `--speaker-threshold` (default 0.45), `--vad-threshold` (default 0.5). Run `--help` for all of them.
- **Long turns are cut every 8 s**, sometimes inside a word: each part is transcribed and translated on its own,
  which costs accuracy (CER 3.3 % on whole sentences vs 5.7 % in the test call). Example: `քարանձավ` (cave) was
  split into "…մի։" and "Անցավ…", so the cave is missing from the English.
- **Two very similar voices** were never tested: the Armenian test data has only one man and two women.
- **Phrase-level output.** Nothing is printed until the speaker pauses (or 8 s pass). Long sentences that are
  force-cut are translated in pieces, so the translation can be worse at the cut.
```

  "content": "\"\"\"Compare translation models: chrF against the official English FLEURS translations, plus latency.\n\n    python eval/run_mt_fleurs.py --nllb-dir models/nllb-200-distilled-600M-ct2-int8\n    python eval/run_mt_fleurs.py --nllb-dir models/nllb-200-distilled-1.3B-ct2-int8\n\nFLEURS sentences have the same id in every language, so hy_am and en_us give Armenian/English pairs. Uses the\nfirst --n sentences of the test split (one recording's text per sentence). Also translates the colloquial\nphrases from the live microphone test (eval/colloquial.tsv) and prints them for a human to judge: no reference\nscore there, because a handful of sentences gives no reliable number.\nAppends one row to eval/results/mt_results.csv; per-sentence output goes to eval/results/mt_<model>.tsv.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport csv\nimport os\nimport sys\nimport time\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom common import RESULTS, ROOT\n\nsys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT))\nfrom livetr.translate import NllbTranslator  # noqa: E402\n\n\ndef fleurs_pairs(n: int) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:\n    from huggingface_hub import hf_hub_download\n\n    def load(lang: str) -> dict[str, str]:\n        path = hf_hub_download(\"google/fleurs\", f\"data/{lang}/test.tsv\", repo_type=\"dataset\")\n        with open(path, encoding=\"utf-8\") as f:\n            return {r[0]: r[2] for r in csv.reader(f, delimiter=\"\\t\", quoting=csv.QUOTE_NONE)}\n\n    hy, en = load(\"hy_am\"), load(\"en_us\")\n    ids = sorted(i for i in hy if i in en)[:n]\n    return [(hy[i], en[i]) for i in ids]\n\n\ndef main() -> None:\n    os.environ.setdefault(\"HF_HUB_OFFLINE\", \"1\")\n    p = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n    p.add_argument(\"--nllb-dir\", required=True)\n    p.add_argument(\"--n\", type=int, default=150)\n    args = p.parse_args()\n    from sacrebleu.metrics import CHRF\n\n    model = Path(args.nllb_dir).name\n    pairs = fleurs_pairs(args.n)\n    tr = NllbTranslator(args.nllb_dir)\n    tr.translate(\"Բարև\")  # warm-up\n    hyps, lats = [], []\n    for hy, _ in pairs:\n        t = time.monotonic()\n        hyps.append(tr.translate(hy))\n        lats.append(time.monotonic() - t)\n    chrf = CHRF().corpus_score(hyps, [[en for _, en in pairs]]).score\n\n    RESULTS.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    with open(RESULTS / f\"mt_{model}.tsv\", \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\", newline=\"\") as f:\n        w = csv.writer(f, delimiter=\"\\t\")\n        w.writerow([\"hy\", \"reference_en\", \"output_en\", \"seconds\"])\n        for (hy, en), out, lat in zip(pairs, hyps, lats):\n            w.writerow([hy, en, out, f\"{lat:.2f}\"])\n    lats.sort()\n    row = dict(model=model, sentences=len(pairs), chrf=f\"{chrf:.1f}\", lat_mean=f\"{sum(lats) / len(lats):.2f}\",\n               lat_p90=f\"{lats[int(len(lats) * 0.9)]:.2f}\", lat_max=f\"{lats[-1]:.2f}\", date=time.strftime(\"%Y-%m-%d %H:%M\"))\n    summary = RESULTS / \"mt_results.csv\"\n    new = not summary.exists()\n    with open(summary, \"a\", newline=\"\") as f:\n        w = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=list(row))\n        if new:\n            w.writeheader()\n        w.writerow(row)\n    print(\"  \".join(f\"{k}={v}\" for k, v in row.items()))\n\n    print(\"\\ncolloquial phrases (live mic test):\")\n    with open(ROOT / \"eval\" / \"colloquial.tsv\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as f:\n        for r in csv.DictReader(f, delimiter=\"\\t\"):\n            print(f\"  HY: {r['hy']}\\n  MT: {tr.translate(r['hy'])}\\n  meant: {r['meaning']}\\n\")\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n"

Building a live Armenian-to-English speech transcription and translation tool.
Building a live Armenian-to-English speech transcription and translation tool.
Before I start, I need a few facts about your laptop. The brief requires everything to run locally on the machine you bring to the interview. The OS determines how system audio gets captured, and the hardware determines which Armenian speech models can stay under 5 seconds. One other thing to know: the evaluators will read these chats to judge whether you steer the AI or just follow it. Working through it step by step with you making the calls will count in your favour.

Which OS will you run this on at the interview?
macOS (Apple Silicon)
What hardware does that laptop have for running models?
Apple Silicon, 8GB RAM
How do you want to work through this?
Plan first, build in steps (Recommended)
Finding an Armenian speech recognition model for limited hardware.
Finding an Armenian speech recognition model for limited hardware.
You’re on Apple Silicon with 8GB. That memory limit will drive most of the model choices, so I’ll check what currently exists for Armenian before writing the plan.

Created a file, shared a file, and 6 more steps
I’ve drafted PLAN.md and haven’t written any code yet. The 8 GB of RAM is the biggest constraint: all models together need to fit in roughly 4.5 GB without swapping, because swapping would push you past the 5-second limit.

What I found about Armenian models:

Zero-shot Whisper is weak on Armenian. On the combined FLEURS + Common Voice test set, BuzzASR/armenian (a fine-tune of whisper-large-v3) reports 2.28% CER versus 15.53% for zero-shot Whisper-large-v3. That makes it the accuracy favourite. 
huggingface
It may be too heavy for your Mac. It’s a large-v3 model of about 3 GB, so it could be too slow or memory-hungry on 8 GB.
The faster alternative is an Armenian fine-tune of Whisper-turbo (Chillarmo/whisper-large-v3-turbo-armenian). I couldn’t find a reliable published error rate for it.
We’ll decide by measuring on your machine, not by trusting the model cards. That’s tip 5 in your guide, and it gives you a strong story for the interview.
For translation, NLLB-200 600M in int8 is the obvious choice. It is commonly reported to need about 3 GB of memory in CTranslate2 format, versus about 5.5 GB for the 1.3B version, so the 600M version fits the budget. 
huggingface
For call-side speakers, the plan is voice embeddings plus online clustering. diart uses this same approach, an incremental clustering algorithm that gets more accurate as the conversation progresses. 
pypi
My workspace has no microphone and probably can’t download the models. So I’ll write the code and unit tests here, and you’ll run the benchmarks and live tests on your Mac, then paste the results back. That split also fits what the evaluators want to see.

To start step 0, I need answers to three things:

Do you approve the plan, or want anything changed?
Very short call-side phrases are hard to attribute to a voice. Should they be labelled Speaker ?, or assigned to the closest known speaker? I’d go with Speaker ?: it’s honest, and the brief rewards honesty over hiding problems.
What does python3 --version print on your Mac, and can you get 1–2 people to record short test clips later this week?

Chillarmo/whisper-large-v3-turbo-armenian
NLLB-200 3.3B CTranslate2 (memory notes)
diart on PyPI

Created 36 files, edited 4 files, and 29 more steps
The code, docs and unit tests are written and committed locally in armenian-live-translate.zip; nothing is pushed to GitHub. The 33 unit tests pass, but the program has never run with real models or real audio, because my workspace can’t reach Hugging Face and has no microphone.

What I could check here: I read the source of each library at the versions in requirements.txt instead of writing the calls from memory. That caught two mistakes I would otherwise have made. The tests also caught a real bug: Armenian question and stress marks (ինչպե՞ս) were splitting words and would have inflated the error rate for every question.

What’s in the repo:

The program: mic and BlackHole capture, voice detection that cuts at pauses, Armenian transcription, English translation, and Me / Speaker N / Speaker ? labels. Each line shows its latency, broken down by stage. --file replays a recording, so you can test without a call.
Measurement scripts that record clips, compare the four transcription models on your own voice, tune the speaker threshold, and save the numbers to eval/results/.
The process files: PLAN.md, DECISIONS.md, TESTS.md and a CLAUDE.md with project rules, plus the README covering all the sections the brief asks for.
Next steps on your Mac, in this order:

Set up: follow README section 1, then run python -m livetr --meter to confirm both the mic and BlackHole show sound.
Download and try it: run python scripts/download_models.py --asr all, then python -m livetr.
Measure: record the test clips with a friend and run the benchmarks. The default transcription model and the speaker threshold are still guesses until these numbers exist.
When you do these, paste me the errors and the output. Expect a few fixes on the first real run.

Also have a native speaker check the 24 test sentences in eval/sentences.tsv, since I wrote them myself. Before you submit, export every chat session into chats/.
